## Document 001: 10 月の金融政策決定会合は利上げ見送りへ 政策金利の２％超えは？

Source PDF: all report pdf/10 月の金融政策決定会合は利上げ見送りへ 政策金利の２％超えは？.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 14 日(水)

1/3 
10 月の金融政策決定会合は利上げ見送りへ  
政策金利の２％超えは？ 
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・10月30日の金融政策決定会合に先立って、早くも観測記事が金融市場を賑わせている。米系
通信は「日銀は今月会合で利上げ見送る公算大、12月の可能性排除せず」と報じた。記事内
では「経済・物価は日銀の見通しに沿って推移しており、２会合連続での利上げが必要なほ
ど物価の上振れリスクが一段と強まっている情勢にはない」、とはいえ「一時的な要因を除
いた基調的な物価上昇率は上昇を続けており、目標の２％から上振れていくリスクには依然
として注意が必要だ」などとする日銀関係者の発言が記述されていた。また既報のとおり、
基調的物価上昇率が概ね２％に達したとの判断を展望レポートに記すことにも触れられてい
た。

・10月の利上げ見送りの根拠として、昨日発表された９月の企業物価指数は一定の存在感があ
る。国内企業物価指数は前月比▲0.3％、前年比＋6.8％と上昇が一服し、市場予想の同＋
7.6％も下回った。原油価格が高止まりするなか、世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の拡大など
を背景に非鉄金属や化学製品などで上昇が続いた一方、米を中心とする農林水産物の価格下
落が下押しに寄与した。円ベースの輸入物価指数は前年比＋24.5％と大幅な上昇を記録した
・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPY は先行き12 ヶ月 155 円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を12 月に 1.5％とした後、27 年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FED はFF 金利を、年内は4.0％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

2/3 
が、８月の同＋25.1％からは小幅に鈍化した。川上段階のインフレ圧力は依然として強いも
のの、９月会合時点の判断に大きな修正を迫るものではなかったと考えられる。

・同日発表された「生活意識に関するアンケート調査」（９月調査）も似たような結果であっ
た。５年後の物価予想については「かなり上がる」の割合が51.1％と前回調査対比で概ね横
ばいとなり、「少し上がる」との合計では86.5％と高止まりした。他方、今後５年間の物価
上昇率（年平均）は、中央値が５％で横ばいも、平均値は＋10.1％と前回調査対比で0.7％pt
低下した。ガソリン代の補助に加え、米価の下落が効いたとみられ、家計の予想物価上昇率
が一段と上放れする事態は回避された。政府による家計支援策が効いているとはいえ、家計
の予想物価上昇率が低下したことは、日銀に時間的余裕を与え得る。なお、景況感について
は「１年前との比較」と「１年後の予想」の双方とも大きな変化は認められなかった。

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
・先行きの金融政策はどうなるであろうか。筆者は、10月会合は利上げ見送り、12月会合で利
上げを決定し、政策金利は1.5％で越年すると予想する。Ｆｅｄの利上げ観測が燻り、円安圧
力が強いなか、日銀が金融引き締め方向への手を緩めるとなれば、一段と円安が進行しかね
ない。また日銀短観（９月調査）を見る限り、企業の金融環境に大きな変化はみられず、既
往の利上げが実体経済に染み渡っている様子はそれほど窺えない。これらを踏まえ、筆者は
2027年も３～４回の利上げがあり、政策金利は２％を超えることを想定している。

・ただし、Ｆｅｄが2027年に政策金利を据え置き、その次の政策変更が利下げになるとの見方
が生じれば、日銀は円安対策から解放され、利上げを中断ないしは終了する可能性がある。
その点、ウィリアムズ・ＮＹ連銀総裁とウォラー理事の発言は注視しておきたい。まずウィ
リアムズ総裁は10月13日に「来年はインフレがかなり鈍化し、通年でみれば、全体のインフ
レ率は目標⁠の２％近辺になるとみている。これは私が言及した要因、すなわち関税の影響が
完全に過去のものとなることを反映している」として、2027年に利上げは必要ないとの見方
を示した。

・⁠ウォラー理事は10月13日に「最近の米国債利回りの上昇は経済成長の加速とＡＩへの大規模
投資によるもので、インフレ期待の高まりが原因ではない」、「インフレ期待は引き続き
『しっかりと安定している』」と述べ、その上で「データセンター建設を含むＡＩ投資が経
済の需要を支えており、今後２～３年はその効果が続く⁠可能性がある。大幅な生産性向上が
実現するのは、なお『数年先』だ」とした。同理事の発言を整理すると、①予想インフレは
安定しており、その点において現在の金融政策は適切である、②ＡＩ投資が一巡すれば、そ
れに起因するインフレ圧力は鈍化する、③その後に生産性向上によるディスインフレ方向へ
の動きが予想される、といったところであろう。

・両氏の予想が現実のものになれば、Ｆｅｄの利上げ観測は後退し、場合によっては2027年に
利下げ観測が台頭することもあり得る。そうした環境下、日銀は利上げペースを緩める、或
いは終了を検討するなど、多くの選択肢を手にすることができる。

## Document 008: 115年9月海關進出口貿易初步統計 (2026-10-08)

115 年 9 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
9 月出口 872.2 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 5.9%，較上年
同月增 60.9%(按新臺幣計算增 68.2%)；第3 季出口 2,449.2 億美元，
較上年同季增 44.6%；累計 1 至 9 月 6,615.5 億美元，較上年同期增
46.2%(按新臺幣計算增 49.1%)。 
（二）進口 
9 月進口 635.9 億美元，亦創歷年單月新高，較上月增 5.8%，較上
年同月增 51.7%(按新臺幣計算增 58.6%)；第 3 季進口 1,818.0 億美
元，較上年同季增 44.3%；累計 1 至 9 月 5,010.0 億美元，較上年同
期增 41.3%(按新臺幣計算增 43.8%)。 
（三）出入超 
9 月出超 236.3 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 113.4 億美元；
累計 1 至 9 月出超 1,605.5 億美元，較上年同期增 625.4 億美元。

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
由於創新科技應用商機持續擴展，推升相關供應鏈出貨動能，加上
消費性電子新品備貨需求與產品價格上漲效應之挹注， 9 月出口較
上年同月增 330.0 億美元(+60.9%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 資通與視聽產品出口 391.3 億美元(歷年單月次高)， 較上年同月增
198.6 億美元(+1.0 倍)， 其中電腦及其附屬單元增157.4 億美元(+1.1
倍)、儲存媒體增 17.6 億美元(+2.2 倍)、交換器及路由器增 10.5
億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
2. 電子零組件出口 311.3 億美元(歷年單月次高)， 較上年同月增97.1
億美元(+45.3%)，其中積體電路增 93.9 億美元(+46.1%)、印刷電
路增 1.4 億美元(+33.7%)、 電容器及電阻器增0.8 億美元(+32.0%)。 
3. 礦產品出口 22.6 億美元，較上年同月增 10.5 億美元(+86.4%)，其
中石油煉製品增 10.1 億美元(+99.0%)。 
4. 機械出口 27.4 億美元(歷年單月次高)，較上年同月增 6.4 億美元
(+30.7%)，其中生產半導體等機械增 2.7 億美元(+61.0%)、未列名
具特殊功能機械增 0.6 億美元(+73.6%)。 
5. 基本金屬及其製品出口 26.9 億美元，較上年同月增 5.0 億美元
(+22.9%)，其中銅及其製品增 2.5 億美元(+65.1%)、鋼鐵及其製品
增 1.4 億美元(+11.7%)。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
隨 AI 產業鏈全球分工與出口引申需求擴增，電子零組件、資通產品
及半導體設備進口維持強勁，加上國際農工原料行情處於高檔，9
月進口較上年同月增 216.7 億美元(+51.7%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口440.5 億美元， 較上年同月增163.9億美元(+59.3%)，
其中電子零組件增 112.6 億美元(+82.5%)、電腦之零附件增 19.4
億美元(+1.5 倍)、礦產品增 17.1 億美元(+38.9%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 128.0 億美元， 較上年同月增 36.0 億美元(+39.1%)，
其中資通設備增 18.4 億美元(+75.0%)、半導體設備增 12.4 億美元
(+34.9%)，交通運輸設備減 1.4 億美元(-46.4%)。 
3. 消費品進口 62.1 億美元，較上年同月增 16.5 億美元(+36.0%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增 16.4 億美元(+5.3 倍)、筆電增 0.5 億
美元(+43.8%)，藥粧用品減 1.2 億美元(-16.6%)。

5 
四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
9 月對東協出口創新高，對美國、陸港為單月次高 
1. 對美國出口 274.6 億美元，較上年同月增 141.5 億美元(+1.1 倍)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 124.1 億美元(+1.3 倍)、 電子零組件增10.1
億美元(+1.3 倍)、機械增 2.2 億美元(+42.3%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 206.2 億美元，較上年同月增 47.8 億美元
(+30.2%)，其中電子零組件增 34.9 億美元(+30.5%)、資通與視聽
產品增 5.7 億美元(+39.0%)、機械增 1.9 億美元(+43.7%)。 
3. 對東協出口 188.8 億美元，較上年同月增 91.2 億美元(+93.4%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 49.7 億美元(+2.2 倍)、電子零組件增 32.0
億美元(+63.3%)、礦產品增 4.8 億美元(+1.4 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 45.3 億美元，較上年同月增 7.7 億美元(+20.6%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 5.7 億美元(+34.1%)、電子零組件增 2.2 億美
元(+56.6%)，基本金屬及其製品減 0.8 億美元(-20.5%)。 
5. 對日本出口 30.7 億美元，較上年同月增 3.6 億美元(+13.4%)，其
中電子零組件增 1.5 億美元(+11.0%)、基本金屬及其製品增 0.6 億
美元(+32.5%)、機械增 0.5 億美元(+29.1%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 9 月對美國出口 2,077.8 億美元，較上年同期增 773.1 億美
元(+59.3%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,600.9 億美元，增 355.3 億美
元(+28.5%)， 對東協出口1,339.6 億美元 ， 增463.6 億美元(+52.9%)，
對歐洲出口 421.8 億美元 ， 增138.4 億美元(+48.8%)， 對日本出口277.7
億美元，增 58.3 億美元(+26.6%)。

七、綜合分析
（一）受惠於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及雲端服務等應用商機持續擴展，
加以步入傳統外銷旺季 、產品價格上漲效應，9 月出口 872.2 億美元，
年增 60.9%，連續 35 個月正成長；進口635.9 億美元，年增51.7%，
主因 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口引申需求擴張，及國際原物料行情居
高；出、進口值及出超 236.3 億美元齊創單月新高。累計 1 至 9 月出
口年增 46.2%、進口增 41.3%。
（二）9 月主要貨品出口普遍較上年同月成長，以資通與視聽產品增 1.0 倍
最佳，包括顯示卡、伺服器、儲存媒體、 交換器及路由器等出口均
呈倍數增加 ；其次為礦產品增 86.4%，主因全球油品供應偏緊支撐價
格維持高檔；電子零組件隨 AI 應用及消費性電子新品備貨需求升溫，
增 45.3%，機械、電機產品亦各增 3 成上下。累計 1 至 9 月資通與視
聽產品、電子零組件出口併計增 59.5%，占總出口比重 79.0%。

（三）主要市場方面，9 月對美國、東協出口值分創歷年單月次高、新高，
年增率各為 1.1 倍及 93.4%，合計對總出口增加貢獻達 7 成；對中國
大陸與香港、歐洲、日本出口各年增 30.2%、20.6%、13.4%。累計 1
至 9 月對五大市場出口值皆為歷年同期最高，其中對美國、東協均
提前刷新全年紀錄 ， 分別年增59.3%、52.9%，對歐洲出口年增48.8%，
對陸港、日本各增 28.5%、26.6%。
（四）展望未來，全球貿易政策及地緣政治情勢變化等，仍增添總體 經濟
不確定性，惟隨人工智慧應用 持續深化，國際雲端服務業者 及各國
加速 AI 基礎建設投資，帶動半導體、伺服器等相關供應鏈需求擴增，
加以科技新品備貨、中國大陸及歐美年底採購旺季將屆， 均有利挹
注我國外銷動能，第 4 季出口可望延續成長態勢。

2026 年獲利亮眼，惟 2027 年供給壓力漸增 
 歐美航線運價優於預期，預估 3Q26 稅後 EPS 19.62 元：3Q26 營收
1,467.76 億元(+51.4%Y oY)，優於預期。受惠運價上漲獲利率提升，
然考量燃油成本占整體營業成本逾 2 成，油價上漲仍對成本形成壓力，
預估稅後淨利 424.74 億元(+95.3%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 19.62 元。 
 受地緣政治及供應鏈重組影響，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 13.24 元：展
望後市，根據 Clarksons Aug -26 預測，2026 年全球貨櫃供給年增
4.6%，需求年增 3.8%，供給增幅仍高於需求，惟供需缺口較 2025 年
逐步收斂。預估 2026 年營收 4,735.4 億元(+24.9%Y oY)，稅後淨利
991.6 億元(+44.6%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 45.8 元。展望 2027 年，隨新船
陸續交付，儘管塞港情況仍可能限縮有效 運力，惟考量整體供給成長
仍高於需求，預期市場運價將逐步回歸常態。整體而言，預估2027 年
營收 4,097.3 億元(-13.5%YoY)，稅後淨利 482.4 億元(-51.4%Y oY)，
稅後 EPS 22.28 元。

(2Q27BVPS PB 0.9X)。

長榮 為 全 球 貨 櫃 運 力 第七大 航
商，市占率 5.9% 
 長榮成立於 1968 年 9 月，實收資本額 216.5 億元。截至 3Q26，全球超過 1.3
萬位員工及 293 個代理行，航線擴及 101 個國家，總船隊數共 242 艘(自有船
比例超過 6 成)，預計至 2030 年仍有 72 艘船待交付。根據 Alphaliner 統計，
截至目前長榮為全球貨櫃運力第七大航商，市佔率約 5.9%。長榮 1H26 營收
結構為貨運收入 93%，船艙租收入 1.2%，其他 5.8%。

歐 美 航 線 運 價 優 於 預 期 ， 預 估
3Q26 稅後 EPS 19.62 元 
 長榮 Sep-26 營收 497.11 億元(+1.48%MoM，+63.75%Y oY)，3Q26 營收達成
率 105.5%，優於預期，主要受惠歐美航線運價走升，且旺季附加費及燃油附
加費挹注營收 。獲利方面 ，受惠運價上漲帶動獲利率提升 ， 惟中東衝突於Jul-26
一度趨緩後再度升溫，且考量燃油成本占整體營業成本逾 2 成，油價上漲仍對
成本形成壓力，預估 3Q26 毛利率 38.91%。由於營收規模擴大，費用率有望
下降 ，預估營益率 35.71%，假設 匯率 持穩 ，預估稅後淨利 424.74 億元
(+164.9%QoQ，+95.3%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 19.62 元。

歐美航線需求放緩 ，預估 4Q26
稅後 EPS 14.89 元 
 根據上海航交所 10/9 公告，上海出口集裝箱運價指數 (SCFI) 3,664.11 點
(+0.05%WoW，+215.76%Y oY)，隨中國十一長假結束，歐線運價短期反彈，
分別為遠東-歐洲線+3.41%WoW，遠東-地中海線+0.2%WoW；美線持平，遠
東-美西線+0.08%WoW，遠東-美東-0.04%WoW。觀察 10/11 公告跨大西洋、
歐 洲 及 跨 太 平 洋 航 線 運 力 分 別 變 動 -1.14%WoW 、 -1.88%WoW 及
+0.48%WoW。整體而言，隨歐美航線逐步進入傳統淡季，航商陸續調整運力
配置，其中歐洲線運力投放減少，美線則大致持平，顯示航商持續透過運力調
節支撐市場運價。

展望 4Q26，需求面而言，歐線隨傳統旺季結束，終端需求動能轉弱，預期後
續運價將呈緩步回落走勢。美線方面，旺季需求已近尾聲，且部分船班裝載率
已有下滑，儘管 Sep-26 川習會後美國調降部分消費品關稅，包括玩具、小型
家電及節慶裝飾品等，惟目前尚未出現明顯大型備貨潮。供給面而言，隨水情
陸續改善 ， 巴拿馬運河管理局預計自2026/10/15 起將每日允許通行量由 32 艘
回升至 33 艘，惟仍低於原先 36 艘水準。考量大型貨櫃船通行具一定優先性，
目前對船期營運影響相對有限，惟通行量尚未完全恢復，仍可能使美東線有效
運力有所收斂。另，美伊衝突及紅海通行風險仍存， Gemini 聯盟持續恢復部
分紅海航線，近期亦傳出 Premier Alliance 有望於 4Q26 恢復部分紅海航線，
若後續其他航商陸續跟進，繞航所吸收之有效運力恐逐步釋放，對市場運價形
成壓力。

整體而言，儘管近洋線進入傳統旺季，惟長榮歐美航線營收占比較高，預期整
體運價及貨量將隨淡季到來而回落 ， 預估4Q26 營收 1,350.87 億元(-8%QoQ，
+57.6%Y oY)。獲利方面，儘管油價仍維持高位，惟隨貨量減少，相關變動成
本亦有望下降，預估毛利率 37.9%。另考量長榮往年於 4Q 提列年終獎金費
用，預期費用率將季增，預估營益率 29.4%。假設匯率持穩，預估稅後淨利
323.53 億元(-23.8%QoQ，+279.8%Y oY)，4Q26 稅後 EPS 14.94 元。

2026 年有效運力供給受限 ， 預估

 展望後市，根據 Clarksons Aug-26 預測，2026 年全球貨櫃供給年增 4.6%，需
求年增 3.8%，供給增幅仍高於需求，惟供需缺口較 2025 年逐步收斂。短期而
言，受到缺工及天候因素影響，部分港口作業效率下降，近期塞港現象增加，
有助吸收部分有效運力。展望 2027 年，在尚未考慮紅海全面復航之前提下，
Clarksons 預估全球貨櫃供給+8.8%Y oY，需求+3.5%YoY，供需缺口將再度擴
大；若紅海航線進一步恢復，繞航所吸收之有效運力亦將逐步釋放，供給壓力
恐進一步增加。需求方面，則須持續關注停戰協議進展、美國關稅政策變化及
通膨壓力對終端需求之影響。

展望 2026 年，長榮持續優化航線布局，強化東南亞、中東、非洲及中南美洲
等區域配置 ， 並透過長約布局降低市場運價波動影響，整體而言 ，元富預估 2026
年營收 4,735.4 億元(+24.9%Y oY)。獲利方面，預估毛利率 30.8%，營益率
25.4%，稅後淨利 991.6 億元(+44.6%Y oY)，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 45.8 元。
展望 2027 年，隨新船陸續交付，全球貨櫃船隊運力持續增加，儘管塞港情況
仍可能限縮有效運力，惟考量整體供給成長仍高於需求，預期市場運價將逐步
回歸常態。整體而言，預估 2027 年營收 4,097.3 億元(-13.5%Y oY)，稅後淨利
482.4 億元(-51.4%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 22.28 元。

### Page 2

For the month of October 2026, we estimate AirTAC’s monthly revenue will decrease 
to Rmb729mn (-10% MoM, +16% YoY)/NT$3,447mn (-10% MoM, +28% YoY) 
We expect October revenue will go down by 10% MoM to Rmb729mn/NT$3,447mn, 
mainly due to 19% fewer working days in Mainland China. However, we expect to see 
improvement in daily pneumatic revenue (Rmb32.3mn in Oct vs. Rmb 29.4mn in Sept) as 
the improvement in daily pneumatic revenue is driven by the strong growth from key 
customers, including electronics, followed by machine tools and battery, and we further 
expect demand to improve following the end of low season.

Earnings and TP revisions 
We revise down our 2026E earnings estimates by 1% to factor in actual 3Q26 revenue 
(revise down 2026 revenue by 1%). We revise down our 2027E/2028E earnings estimate 
by 1%/1% to factor in a more conservative assumption on battery and linear guide 
business (revise down AirTAC revenue by 1%/1% in 2027E/2028E). We continue to see 
the company focusing on cost control as well as experiencing a better product mix 
outlook, but we expect some offset by rising material cost (revise down 2026E/2028E 
OPM by 0.1/0.1ppt).

### Page 3

Maintain our Buy rating on AirTAC, with a new 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925), 
based on an unchanged target P/E of 26x on 2027 EPS.

Investment view: Accelerating monthly YoY from smaller segments could be a surprise. 
Maintain positive view on AirTAC 
We reiterate our Buy rating on AirTAC as we continue to believe the company is a key 
beneficiary of the factory automation/industrial automation localization trend in 
mainland China, and we expect the company will also benefit from the prolonged 
upcycle supported by the ongoing government stimulus policies. We see multiple key 
end-segments’ performance tracking ahead of the company’s 2026 full year guidance of 
20%+ YoY growth (see here), with battery/textile/machine tools/electronics delivering 
55%/46%/41%/23% YTD YoY growth while total revenue in Rmb is tracking at 23% YTD 
YoY growth. 
We continue to see solid monthly YoY growth from smaller segments, such as energy 
lighting and textile. However, the battery segment, which is the key growth driver in 
2025 (with 89% YoY in 2025), is seeing its first negative monthly YoY growth since Oct 
2024, while other major contribution sectors, such as electronics and automotive are 
seeing decelerating monthly YoY growth. As a result, we remain cautious on the 
potential of exaggerated orders (including advance or duplicate orders) despite new 
orders intake continuing to outpace shipments, and we will continue to monitor the 
underlying end-demand closely.

### Page 4

Maintain Buy on AirTAC with a lower 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925). 
Investment Thesis - AirTAC 
AirTAC is the 2nd-largest pneumatic supplier in mainland China with 28% market share 
in 2025, outpacing most local competitors, owing to its low-cost yet high quality 
product, and service quality reputation in most key cities in mainland China. We forecast 
its market share will reach 30% in 2026E and expect AirTAC to be the structural winner 
in a deflationary market, due to its: (1) strong market growth and leadership: AirTAC 
continues to expand its market share in mainland China driven by competitive pricing 
that is more than 10% lower vs. international peers (with 45%+ GM / 30-35% OPM) and 
high demand elasticity among mainland China customers; and (2) superior cost structure 
and financial health: with best-in-class cash cost and a solid balance sheet, featuring 
positive net cash over FCF since 2023/2024, we believe AirTAC is well-equipped to 
weather industry downturns and capitalize on market restructuring. 
Overall, we believe AirTAC will see better revenue/profitability in the long term given its 
share gainer position in the China pneumatic market and potential to gain share in the 
linear guide market (improved demand in the next 1-2 years). This should lead to 
strengthened valuation, vs. its recent trading range (trading below its 10-year avg. P/E 
multiple). We are Buy rated on AirTAC. 
Price target risks and methodology - AirTAC 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$1,895 is based on a target P/E of 26.0x on 
our 2027E EPS (0.25x STDV above avg of the last pneumatic upcycle). 
Key downside risks: (1) Stronger competition from peers; (2) sharper-than-expected 
Rmb depreciation, and (3) a longer-than-expected end-market demand downtrend in 
mainland China.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 12 Oct 2026 close.

and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

at NT$6,800

outlook for 4Q26 thanks to AI ASIC contribution and better mix/pricing for
consumer products. On the back of solid operating leverage, we are also looking for

OPM upside. We are looking for upbeat 4Q26 guidance.

years, we expect OPM expansion for Mediatek on the back of its operational
leverage. We lift our earnings projection for 2026/2027 /28 by 9%/ 25%/9%. We
maintain our Buy with TP$6,800 (30x of 2027 /28 EPS, from 35x previously, mainly
on recent macro concerns and concerns on AI capex beyond 2028).

### Page 7

Mediatek (2454.TW)
21 October 2026 Citi Research
7
Mediatek
Company description
Mediatek (MTK) is a fabless IC company that designs SoC for smartphone,
smart TV, and digital media devices. It also develops chips for connectivity,
networking, smart home gadgets, and automotive sectors. It was founded in
1997, is listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange, and is headquartered in Taiwan.
It has sales and research subsidiaries in mainland China, Singapore, India,

the US, Japan, Korea, Denmark, and England. It primarily sells its products in
China and other emerging-market countries.
Investment strategy
We rate Mediatek shares as Buy. We expect Mediatek to ride on 5G migration
in emerging markets and market share gain of its high-end Dimensity series in
China market as well. In particular, we see strong growth potential from its AI
ASIC, NTN-satellite business in the long term. We believe solid fundamentals

and strong operating leverage will support Mediatek’s margins outlook.
Valuation
Our NT$6,800 target price (rounded) for Mediatek is based on 30x P/E to
2027 /2028 EPS, which is 1-std above its five-year average forward P/E given
the promising outlook from its AI ASIC business starting from 2H26. We
believe our target PE is also justified by Mediatek's global and regional peers'
trading at an average of c32x PER. AI ASIC development with CSPs should

provide more upside from 2027 onwards. We also see Mediatek as well
prepared for 6G and 6G+NTN Satellite communication. Despite lukewarm
smartphone overall deamand, we expect Mediatek to benefit from 5G
migration in emerging markets and its Dimensity series should also gain
shares in flagship models. Non-smartphone business is also seeing
stabilizing TV and Io T demand with Mediatek outgrowing the overall market
on its better technology in WiFi 7 and connectivity.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth from AI ASIC business; 2)
more severe competition in the smartphone segment; 3) slower overall
demand related to macroeconomic risks, especially for smartphone demand;
and 4) further price hikes from foundries.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.
Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst

expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment
rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient

valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating
and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of

information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price
chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and

investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the
expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.

Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:
Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in

reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

## Document 203: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대단기 부담_2a8cdc97

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대단기 부담_2a8cdc97.pdf

제약업종

◼ 3Q26 컨센서스 부합, 4Q26 성장폭 확대 전망 
◼ 5공장 Ramp-up 지속, 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용)

3Q26 Preview: 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 전망 
3Q26 매출액 1조 3,380억원(+6.4% yoy, +1.3% qoq), 영업이익 5,890억원(-
7.0% yoy, +0.4% qoq, OPM 44.0%)으로 컨센서스에 부합하는 실적을 전
망함. 3분기 평균 원/달러 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락하고, 지난 5월 
파업에 따른 생산 차질 영향이 반영되나, 5공장 PPQ 물량의 매출 인식이 
시작되며 yoy, qoq 외형 성장은 이어질 것으로 예상함.  
파업 당시 생산이 지연된 물량은 3Q 중 추가 생산되어 4Q26부터 매출에 반
영될 전망임. 5공장은 다수 프로젝트의 PPQ가 진행 중이며, Ramp-up에 
따라 매출 기여도 점진적으로 확대될 것으로 예상함. 이에 4Q26에는 파
업 이연 물량 반영과 5공장 매출 확대에 따라 실적 성장폭이 확대될 전
망임. 2026년 연간 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 5조 5,310억원(+21.4% 
yoy), 2조 4,780억원(+19.8% yoy, OPM 44.8%)으로 전망함.

신규 수주 회복 기대, 중장기 성장 기반은 확대 중 
관세 관련 불확실성으로 둔화됐던 고객사와의 수주 논의가 재개되고 있음. 
10월 2일 유럽 소재 제약사와 935억원 규모의 CMO 계약을 추가 체결
했으며, 4Q26 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목함.  
PolyPeptide 인수는 연내 거래 종결을 목표로 진행 중이며, 인수 완료 
시 펩타이드 분야로 CDMO 포트폴리오가 확대될 전망임. 6 공장도 연내 
착공을 목표로 준비 중임. 
한편, 노사 리스크는 단기 불확실성 요인임. 노조는 10/26부터 3주간 2
차 총파업을 예고함. 1차 대비 예정된 파업 기간이 길어진 만큼 실제 파
업 진행 시 생산 차질 규모가 주요 변수임. 다만 파업 개시 전까지 협상 
가능성이 열려 있어, 향후 노사 협상 진행 경과를 확인할 필요가 있음.

## Document 309: FX Daily_ Defensive rotation in play

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ Defensive rotation in play.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

It looks like markets are going through a subtle regime shift.
Higher energy prices no longer mean higher rates forever;
instead, the long end is starting to express a little more fear
about growth. The upcoming US midterms may also be playing
a role. It looks like there is some defensive rotation underway
into the Swiss franc, the yen and sterling

Chris Turner, Frantisek Taborsky and Francesco Pesole
As the US midterms approach, investors appear to be rotating into defensive
currencies
USD: Midterms could be starting to play
a role
Risk assets are on the back foot this European morning as energy
prices stay ﬁrm and tech stocks remain fragile. On the former, a
hefty risk premium remains in oil prices as attacks against Saudi
Arabia continue. On the latter, Q3 results from Meta and Tesla and
what they mean for capex plans are still being digested, but there
clearly remains a ﬁne line to walk. The suspicion now is that global
equity investors are starting to scale back their exposure to the tech

sector and looking for more defensive plays in sectors like
consumer staples, healthcare and utilities. This will favour more
geographic allocation to European equities, where tech weights in
benchmark indices are lower. The upcoming US midterms in early
November may also play a role here, where a Democrat sweep of
Congress could slow/reverse deregulation.
At the same time, we are seeing a subtle change in the rate
markets. During the late summer and especially in September,

higher energy prices were associated with 'higher rates forever'. The
one-month USD OIS priced 10 years forward traded at 5.17% –
miles away from any neutral rate near 3.50%. These views are now
changing. That OIS pricing has now dropped back to 4.85% as yield
curves have ﬂattened and investors seem to be a little more
worried about growth than inﬂation.
This environment probably favours a little more outperformance of
the defensive currencies over commodity currencies. But until the

rock-solid short-end of the US yield curve starts to buckle, it is hard
to make the case that the dollar needs to fall across the board.
We favour consolidation over correction for the DXY dollar index,
but do not rule out some further softening to the 101.25/30 area.
The US data calendar is light again and we expect the dollar to be
dragged around by tech stocks.
Chris Turner
EUR: Hard to be constructive
EUR/USD is trading quite well despite higher energy prices and an

unresolved French budget crisis. One can only conclude that the
biggest market driver at the moment is the heavy and
concentrated positioning in US tech and the dollar, which is being
gently pared back. Expect FX traders to keep a very close watch on
the short end of the US curve now. Expectations for further Fed
tightening have stayed rock-solid at around +75bp over recent
weeks. We think that could remain the case for the coming months
and that the dollar could stay strong. But any tech wobble that

GBP: Back in fashion
It seems like sterling is riding on the coat-tails of PM Andy
Burnham's popularity. In reality, it looks like there is both
independent sterling strength and independent euro weakness at
play in EUR/GBP. Earlier this summer, we had felt that sterling gains
would not last. But now greater market conﬁdence in a Bank of
England tightening cycle – we look for 25bp hikes in February and

November – makes sterling a high-carry European currency.
Next week's UK budget looks unlikely to derail the recent EUR/GBP
break below 0.8400. And with the euro still having plenty of room to
drop on French politics, EUR/GBP can stay oﬀered in a 0.8360-
0,8430 range for the time being.
Chris Turner
TRY: Easing resumes as carry trade holds
We expect the Central Bank of Turkey to resume its easing cycle for
the ﬁrst time since the US-Iran conﬂict began today, cutting rates

by 100bp to 36.00%. September’s inﬂation data provided a positive
signal, while developments in investment funds point to downside
risks for the economy in the ﬁnal quarter. The authorities’ swift
response appears to have contained the fund shock, with no signs
of systemic risk or a sharp rise in dollarisation, while foreign inﬂows
have resumed.

## Document 324: Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO_.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO?
Date:    6 hours ago
Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Irrational Exuberance In S&P
500 FEMO?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 21 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Chris Geirman / Unsplash
Are analysts' earnings expectations for the S&P 500 irrationally
exuberant? So far this year, FEMO (fabulous earnings momentum, as
measured by actual earnings per share) has been soaring, while FOMO
(fear of missing out, as reflected in P/E multiples) has been mostly
retreating. Much of that earnings optimism is concentrated in the S&P 500
Information Technology sector, particularly the Semiconductors industry,

### Page 3

where the AI investment boom has fueled extraordinary demand, pricing
power, and profit margins. But can these conditions persist? Let's take a
closer look:
(1) FEMO overwhelming FOMO. The S&P 500 is up 13.3% ytd, despite a
13.5% contraction in its forward P/E. That's because FEMO has
overwhelmed waning FOMO, with forward earnings jumping 31.0% since
the start of the year (chart).
The divergence is even more striking in Information Technology. The

sector's forward earnings has soared 65.5% ytd, while its forward P/E has
plunged 25.9%. The S&P 500 IT stock price index has risen 22.6% ytd,
powered by FEMO (chart).

### Page 4

Industry analysts remain remarkably bullish on the S&P 500's
fundamentals. Forward revenues is up 15.7% y/y, while forward earnings
is up 39.0% (chart).
(2) Semiconductors driving FEMO. Much of that optimism originates in
the IT sector, where forward revenues and earnings are up 57.2% and
91.4% y/y, respectively (chart).
Digging deeper reveals that the sector's extraordinary FEMO originates
largely in the Semiconductors industry, where forward revenues and

earnings are up 121.7% and 176.0% y/y, respectively (chart).

### Page 5

Indeed, semiconductors now account for a record 55.0% of the IT sector's
forward earnings and 40.9% of its market capitalization (chart).
The growing importance of semiconductors extends to the broader
market. Semiconductors now account for a record 20.4% of the S&P 500’s
forward earnings and 15.8% of its market capitalization (chart).

### Page 6

That makes the industry's earnings trajectory increasingly important for
the entire index. Analysts expect Semiconductor revenues and earnings to
grow 61.3% and 74.0% over the next year, respectively. More remarkably,
they expect earnings growth to average 66.1% annually over the next five
years.
These extraordinary expectations help explain why analysts project IT
sector earnings growth of 44.8% over the next year and 42.0% annually
over the next five years (chart).

In turn, analysts expect S&P 500 earnings to grow 19.0% over the next
year and an average of 26.1% annually over the next five years, the latter
approaching a record high.

### Page 7

(3) Pricing power fueling Semiconductors' FEMO. To determine
whether S&P 500 FEMO shows irrational exuberance, we need to assess
whether Semiconductor FEMO does so. Much of the industry's
extraordinary earnings growth reflects unprecedented profit margin
expansion. The industry’s forward profit margin nearly doubled from the
2023 lows to a record 51.8% during the week of October 20 (chart).
The explanation is straightforward: Demand for chips has surged as

hyperscalers and neocloud providers have raced to build AI infrastructure,
while supply is capacity constrained. The resulting pricing pressures are
clearly evident in the PPI for electronic components & accessories (up
26.8% y/y through September) and the import price index for computer &
electronic product manufacturing (up 12.4% y/y through September)
(chart).

### Page 8

(4) Will Semiconductors' pricing power persist? That's the critical
question for assessing whether Semiconductor FEMO is irrationally
exuberant. Investors already seem skeptical. The Semiconductor
industry's forward P/E has plunged to just 14.2 despite surging earnings
expectations (chart). In other words, unlike the tech bubble of 1999, any
irrational exuberance in FEMO has been mostly offset by depressed
FOMO!
The goods news is that demand for chips should remain strong. The nine

largest cloud service providers are expected to spend $957 billion on
capex this year, up 106% y/y, and more than $1.5 trillion in 2027. But
supply is starting to catch up. Global semiconductor manufacturing
capacity is expected to expand 5% this year and another 6% in 2027,
potentially easing shortages and price pressures.
(5) Irrational earnings exuberance? We suspect there is some irrational
exuberance in Semiconductor FEMO, particularly in analysts' consensus

long-term earnings growth expectations. Today's demand-supply
imbalance supports extraordinary profits, but sustaining current pricing
power and margins over a longer time horizon seems optimistic as
capacity expands. With Semiconductors accounting for more than one-
fifth of S&P 500 forward earnings, that exuberance is reflected in the
broader index.
Nevertheless, FEMO remains robust beyond Semiconductors. Analysts
expect Q3 earnings growth of 30.7% y/y, with positive growth across all 11

sectors (chart).

### Page 9

Our diffusion indexes also show that an increasing share of S&P 500
companies is experiencing rising forward revenues and earnings (chart).
Combined with expectations that semiconductor demand will continue to
outpace supply, at least over the near term, this suggests that FEMO
remains fundamentally rational.
💡
Join the discussion with Dr. Ed below! Paid members can now
leave comments or questions at the end of our QuickTakes, and it
may be featured in the next "Just Ask Dr. Ed!"

### Page 10

clause.

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZドル相場の行方を左右する総選挙とRBNZの政策運営 ～インフレ圧力は根強い一方、政権交代に伴う政策枠組みの変更が不透明要因に～.pdf

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 22 日

1/3 
NZ ドル相場の行方を左右する総選挙と RBNZ の政策運営 
～インフレ圧力は根強い一方、政権交代に伴う政策枠組みの変更が不透明要因に～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ニュージーランドでは、インフレ高進を背景に RBNZ が９月に２会合連続の利上げを実施し
たものの、11 月７日の総選挙を巡る不透明感がＮＺドル相場の重しとなってきた。 新興政党
のオポチュニティ党が連立協議のカギを握る可能性が高まっているうえ、仮に左派・労働党
を中心とする政権への交代が実現すれば、RBNZ の政策目標に雇用の最大化が再び加えられる
と見込まれる。そのため、追加利上げのハードルが高まる可能性が意識されている。 
○ ７-９月のインフレ率は前年比＋3.7％と鈍化し、市場予想も下回ったものの、引き続き目標
レンジを上回っている。エネルギーや食料品を中心にインフレ圧力が後退する一方、サービ
ス物価には上昇圧力が広がる兆しがうかがえる。さらに、原油価格の高止まりや干ばつによ
る食料インフレの懸念も残る。金融市場では早期利上げ観測がくすぶるものの、今回の物価
統計を受けて見方が修正される可能性もあり、先行きのＮＺドル相場は総選挙の結果と、そ
の後のRBNZ の政策枠組みに関する議論に左右される展開が予想される。

【RBNZは追加利上げ実施も、総選挙を巡る不透明感がNZドル相場の重しとなる展開が続いた】 
ニュージーランドでは、RBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）が９月の定例理事会において２会合
連続の利上げを決定した （注１） 。 背景には、2025 年以降のインフレ率が加速してRBNZ の定める目
標レンジ（１～３％）の上限を上回る伸びで推移していることがある。さらに、イラン情勢の悪化
による原油価格の上昇がエネルギーインフレを招き、４-６月のインフレ率は前年比＋4.1％と2023
年 10-12 月以来の高い伸びとなった。こうした事情は、RBNZ の断続的な利上げ実施を後押ししたと
考えられる。 
一方、通貨ＮＺドル相場は上値の重い展開をみせてきた。同国では、11 月７日に議会（代議院：
一院制 （総議席数120） ） の総選挙の実施が予定されており、選挙結果を巡る不透明感が影響してい
るとみられる。世論調査によれば、右派の国民党、ないし左派の労働党を中心とする連立政権がい
ずれも多数派を形成できず、新興政党・オポチュニティ党がキャスティングボートを握る可能性が

注１ ９月２日付レポート「RBNZ は緩やかな引き締め示唆､NZ ドル相場の行方はどうなる？」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/654752.html）

ーインフレが長引く可能性が高まっている。さらに、エルニーニョ現象の発生を受けて、同国では
東部や北部を中心に、乾燥・干ばつによる農業生産への悪影響が懸念されており、食料インフレや
景気の下押しが懸念される状況にある。 
総選挙においてはインフレも主要な争
点となっており、早期のインフレ抑制に

## Document 349: Open mouth operations (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Open mouth operations (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
Open mouth operations
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
13:34 October 23 2026 Share
The RBA does not want to promise what it will do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks.
Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for every nuance. Lately, there seems to have
been more RBA communication to pore over. The method of delivery has also evolved. This has

### Page 2

Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks for clues to future rate moves. They will
have plenty to pore over in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry of fireside chats
this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday.
Next Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before Senate Estimates, just as the
quarterly CPI is released. (This timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After the

decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a media release and press conference. More
speeches will follow, including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just following the RBA Review but also more
recently. There may be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication outside that certainly
feels more frequent than a couple of years ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little

sparse outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary appearances. We have counted
58 public engagements so far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website. This compares
with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in 2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events were related to economics, markets
and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed as well. There are fewer set-piece

speeches on policy and a lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few years ago, there
are more interviews across a wide range of media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It
was a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the 7:30 Report; it is hard to imagine him
agreeing to an Assistant Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general audience. Central banks want to explain

why they do what they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising but the household
sector is not the source of the inflation.
Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief
Communication Officer highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting media conference is
designed to speak to the public. Market participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
sometimes caused confusion when tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different tone and

varying complexity.
The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly using speeches directed to professional
audiences to discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such as neutral rates and
financial conditions. They are also highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more ways
than just squeezing mortgage holders.
One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises

about the future path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running commentary about how it
is interpreting recent data. These interpretations are themselves useful signals about what the RBA
thinks it might do.

### Page 3

the primary audience. So if you think that what the Governor said in the media conference sounded
more ‘dovish’ than what another official says in a more market-oriented forum, it is partly because she
is trying to convey that the RBA understands that its recent policy actions are making things harder for
many Australians. But this is in much the same way that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It
does, but that will not stop the treatment.

A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted material such as the minutes and set-
piece speeches than in unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be careful with their
words in unscripted settings. If the tone differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have reduced the pressure to make the set-piece

speeches more accessible. Research on central bank communication often uses measures such as
the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was
beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison suggests four speeches from 2026 (1, 2, 3,
4) were harder to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches scored in the 11 to 12
range, which is high-school-level complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and 15, which is

academic or technically dense text requiring university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-Governor Phil Lowe also scored better
on these metrics than the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that readability metrics do
not necessarily imply better clarity if the concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has

begun to put more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those invisible ‘star’ variables
(NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences
has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on the full range of transmission channels from
interest rates to inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour supply channel). This is

understandable in a world of supply shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt they would be comforted, though, by the
Deputy Governor’s explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the smallest of the
transmission channels from interest rates to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on inflation. It raises the question of whether

other policy tools or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on inflation through the other
channels without hitting a minority of households disproportionately.
We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases to summarise the position. From ‘narrow
path’ to ‘not ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once entrenched, a phrase can
outlast its usefulness.

### Page 4

Related articles
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record the presentations to the Board and the
members’ discussion. The minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-meeting
statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The
August 2024 minutes were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how sensitive the forecasts
were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed

Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s communication have not changed. It
wants to explain – and perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how it interprets
incoming data, especially inflation, the labour market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ about
future moves. Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular future path for interest

rates. Some academic literature argues that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high, for
that matter) independently amplifies the transmission of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-
period interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield curve. Central banks including the RBA
have certainly tried jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view, though, is that the
potential cost of reputational loss if things do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a

slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides the signal about future policy directions. This
is why it was noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour market as ‘a little tight’ after the
September meeting, even though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We have been
noting for some time that the RBA staff have a downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the

RBA’s oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not
surprise us.

Charts of the week: Watching earnings and a potential ‘Blue wave’

US Big Tech earnings, mid-term elections and major central bank policy meetings are the near-term market drivers 
Consensus S&P500 index Q3 earnings estimates by sector   Chance of Democrats winning control of US House and Senate*

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *Polymarket probability

Editorial 
Policy, politics and earnings  
Strategy summary:  We retain our near -term defensive and 
diversified stance across assets, sectors and regions amid 
near-term uncertainty around monetary policy, politics and 
earnings. The next two weeks will be busy for major central 
banks, US political watchers and technology leaders powering 
the AI supercycle. We expect the Fed, ECB and BoJ to hold 
rates next week, but markets will focus on whether central 
banks are turning more hawkish due to the latest surge in fuel 
prices. The US mid -term elections are expected to give 
Democrats control of the House and Senate, with implications 
for taxes and the AI-led investment boom.  
Meanwhile, the consensus is expecting the US tech sector to 
report over 60% y/y earnings growth for Q3, the biggest jump 
in over two decades outside the post-COVID rebound.  Each of 
these three drivers have the potential to surprise markets.  
Big Three to hold monetary policy: We expect the Fed, ECB 
and BoJ to hold policy rates next week, after September’s 
hikes, as they assess whether higher bond yields have 
tightened financial conditions enough to offset fuel-price-driven 
inflation. We expect the Big Three to hike next in December as 
fuel prices are likely to stay elevated into early next year due to 
refining capacity constraints. Nevertheless, the Fed and ECB 
are likely to deliver less than around 60bps of rate hikes priced 
in by money markets over the next 12 months on the back of 
an eventual easing of oil and fuel prices leading to disinflation 
by H2 2027. This outlook means 3 -7-year Developed Market 
investment grade bonds currently offer attractive risk-reward.  
Opportunity in Australian bonds. Other major central banks, 
such as the Bank of England, Bank of Canada and Reserve 
Bank of New Zealand are likely to hike rates by 25bps in the 
next two weeks given their focus on taming near-term inflation. 
However, we believe the Reserve Bank of Australia (RBA) is 
close to the end of its hiking cycle, even if it hikes one last time 
in the coming months (money markets give a 60% chance of a 
hike on 3 November). Australia’s domestic labour and housing 
markets and business confidence are softening after the RBA 
hiked its policy rate four times this year to a 15 -year high. 
Meanwhile, Australia’s cash rate is 60bps higher than the US 
policy rate, supporting the AUD. We continue to see attractive 
income and FX gain opportunities from AUD corporate bonds.  
A ‘Blue Wave’ in the US?  Prediction markets strongly favour 
Democrats taking the House of Representatives and possibly 
the Senate in the 3 November US mid-term elections. While an 
opposition sweep in the mid-terms has historically delivered the 
best 12-month returns for equities  among all sc enarios, partly 
due to the resulting policy gridlock, a decisive Democratic “blue 
wave” could increase longer -term risks of higher taxes and 
uncertainty over policy towards AI investment and data centres. 
With the Senate race still close and prediction markets fa llible, 
we remain cautious on risk assets in the near term.  
Closing EU bank contingent convertible bond s (CoCos1) 
and US 10 -year go vernment bond ideas:  We lock in 2% 
gains in EU CoCos and 1.7% gain in US 10 -year government 
bond ideas amid French and US political risks (page 5-6). We 
continue to hedge any inflation risks through inflation-protected 
bonds and geopolitical risk through an Overweight in gold.  
Watching Big Tech earnings:   Upcoming Big Tech earnings 
raises uncertainty as market leadership remains concentrated 
and share -price reactions more mixed. With valuations less 
likely to drive returns, earnings delivery and sustainability of AI 
investment, funding and monet isation and broadening out of 
earnings beyond tech will be critical, raising the risk of volatility 
and sector divergence.  We remain invested but defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification across sectors and regions.  
Staying defensive and diversified. We recently upgraded the 
defensive US Healthcare sector to Overweight , while retaining 
our Overweights to US tech and materials as well as financials 
in the US, Euro area and Japan. We also downgraded the more 
volatile Communications Services sector to Neutral  and took 
profits on the semiconductor segment earlier this month before 
the latest correction. Our defensive and diversified stance 
should preserve participation in further equity upside, while 
reducing exposure to earnings disappointments and supporting 
resilience through the reporting season.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions 
  What are the key takeaways from the technology sector 
earnings reported so far this week? 
Our view: Recent  technology sector earnings results point to 
strong compute demand and continued capex expansion, 
supporting our positive long -term view on the AI investment 
theme. However, rising funding and execution risks warrant 
near-term caution. We prefer to avoid increasing exposure to 
higher-risk areas within the technology sector for now. 
Rationale: Earnings results reported this week suggest major tech 
companies remain committed to expanding their AI infrastructure. 
While this bodes well for AI infrastructure providers, ambitious 
expansion plans imply that an increasing share of AI capex is likely 
to depend on debt financing. We expect debt-financed AI capex to 
rise from c.25% of AI capex in 2026 to c.40% in 2027.  
Greater reliance on borrowing increases sensitivity to interest rates, 
while deployment delays could postpone revenue recognition amid 
ongoing financing costs. However, evidence of improving AI 
monetisation and adoption should strengthen operating cash 
flows and support internal capex funding. Against this backdrop, 
we will assess Big Tech earnings next week for greater clarity on 
capex guidance and funding and AI monetisation trends.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Euro  area bank equities amid 
heightened political and fiscal uncertainty in the region? 
Our view: We remain positive on Euro  area bank equities, 
supported by resilient earnings and shareholder-friendly capital 
return policies. While Fr ance’s fiscal uncertainty and a softer 
growth outlook warrant caution, the recent pullback in Euro  
area bank equities  offers selective opportunities. We favour 
well-capitalised banks with strong deposit franchises and 
diversified earnings. 
Rationale: We expect Euro  area banks’ Q3 2026 net interest 
income to grow 1.5 -3.0% q/q , led by Spanish banks, driven by 
higher rates and continued asset repricing. Resilient fee income from 
wealth management and investment banking activity, as well as 
dividends and buybacks, should provide further support. Price-to-
book (P/B) valuations have eased , despite expectations for 
healthy return on equity (ROE), thus improving risk-reward. 
Fiscal uncertainty in France has widened sovereign spreads, 
pressuring banks’ bond holdings and funding costs . Although 
French banks represent 13% of the Euro  area Bank Index, volatility 
could spread to regional financial markets. However, stress in the 
broader banking system appears limited , supported by strong 
capital buffers and ECB tools to contain disorderly market moves and 
sovereign market contagion. We will continue to monitor credit 
trends, sovereign spreads and banks’ 2027 earnings guidance. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What has driven the recent weakness in  China equities, 
and what is your outlook from here? 
Our view: We maintain China equities as  a Core holding , as 
valuations remain inexpensive. Rapid technological innovation 
and investment in  AI localisation provide tailwinds, but the 
potential for a re-rating appears delayed. Investors should seek 
to diversify their portfolios and reduce concentration risk. 
Rationale: China equities have weak ened, driven by mounting 
scepticism over AI capex returns, structural margin 
compression and concerns about the country’s significant 
financing requirements. Another notable feature in China has been 
significant share issuance, which has diluted earnings per share and 
weighed on the MSCI China Index’s performance. These pressures 
have been compounded by geopolitical headwinds, restrictions on 
advanced chips, an enduring property sect or contraction and weak 
domestic demand. September’s stimulus measures also 
disappointed markets in terms of their scale and pace of delivery. 
Notwithstanding these headwinds, valuations remain inexpensive, 
supported by rapid domestic innovation and structural tailwinds 
in AI localisation , leading us to retain China equities as a Core 
holding. Elevated volatility reinforces the need to be defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification rather than relying on technology 
sector concentration. We also remain Overweight China’s 
healthcare, financial and materials sectors. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Are you still bullish on US 10-year government bonds?   
Our view: We are closing our opportunistic bullish idea on US 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. The risk -reward 
has become less compelling after the yield broke below 5%, the 
lower bound of our three-month forecast range of 5.00-5.25%. 
Rationale: The US 10 -year yield fell  over 17bps to 4.97% on 22 
October – its first move below 5% since 23 September and biggest 
one-day drop since March 2023. A sell-off in AI-related equities and 
renewed Middle East tensions drove investors towards safe havens. 
Earlier this month, the yield hit its highest level in over two decades. 
The forces behind the rally are fragile, though.  Oil prices remain 
elevated amid escalating Middle East tensions, and September US 
consumer inflation rose to 3.7% y/y . Fed officials  have signalled 
there is no urgency to raise rates at the 27-28 October meeting, 
but persistent price pressures keep further tightening on the table. 
We prefer to lock in gains and close our opportunistic bullish idea on 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO, Fixed Income & FX

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for French bank bonds , especially 
ahead of France’s upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews?   
Our view: The recent sell-off in French bank bonds is a repricing 
of tail risks, not a credit story. We expect further price volatility 
until the April 2027 French presidential election, particularly at 
the junior end of the capital structure, so we are closing our 
opportunistic EU bank contingent convertibles (CoCos1) idea at 
a gain of 2.0%. 
Rationale: We believe the recent sell -off in French bank bonds 
reflects domestic political and fiscal risks rather than weaker 
credit fundamentals . The sustained widening of the OAT -Bund 
spread has raised concerns about the profit and loss (P&L) impact 
on banks' sovereign debt holdings. Banks provide limited 
disclosure on these exposures. However, banks’ investment 
portfolios are generally diversified, and holdings booked at amortised 
cost should soften the P&L impact. Near -term catalysts include the 
upcoming sovereign credit rating rev iews. A sovereign downgrade 
could trigger bank rating downgrades, with domestically focused 
lenders potentially more exposed than regionally diversified peers. A 
sovereign credit event is not our base case, but we expect headline 
risks to persist until the  French presidential election . Against this 
backdrop, we are closing our opportunistic EU bank CoCos idea. 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Will the November US mid-term elections and European 
bond volatility support a sustained Swiss franc (CHF) rebound?  
Our view: We expect a near -term CHF rebound, supported 
mainly by safe -haven demand amid European bond volatility. 
We believe the US mid-term elections will be a secondary driver. 
Rationale: European sovereign bond volatility provides the stronger 
catalyst for CHF strength. The Fr ance-Germany government bond 
yield spread remains elevated near 160bps, reflecting heightened 
French fiscal concerns and reinforcing the CHF’s safe-haven appeal. 
The US mid-terms could provide additional support for the CHF. 
A Democratic victory in either chamber is expected to  constrain 
further fiscal expansion, potentially weighing on the USD. However, 
this would not necessarily lead to faster fiscal consolidation. 
The SNB’s 0% policy rate leaves  the CHF at a sizeable interest -
rate disadvantage vs. the USD, constraining sustained CHF gains. 
With EUR/CHF below 0.90 , excessive CHF strength could also 
increase the risk of SNB intervention . A decisive USD/CHF break 
below 0.8130 would strengthen the technical case for a more 
sustained CHF rebound.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

A widening France-Germany 10-year government 
bond yield spread and a stable investment-grade 
(IG) bond option-adjusted spread (OAS) suggest a 
repricing of political and fiscal risk rather than 
credit risk

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would worsening budget deficits and political uncertainty 
across parts of Europe affect your view on EUR/USD and Euro 
area government bond yields? 
Our view: Fiscal and political concerns constrain near -term 
EUR/USD gains, with France being the primary vulnerability. We 
expect EUR/USD to recover towards the 1.17-1.18 range over 
the next  12 months, supported by continued Euro area 
economic growth and an expected fading of USD support. We 
expect wider differentials in Euro area government bond yields, 
with political risk premium elevated in Fr ench government 
bonds (OATs) and German government bonds (Bunds) 
supported by safe-haven demand. 
Rationale: France's high  public debt, large budget deficit and 
political obstacles to fiscal adjustment s are  weighing on market 
confidence, with the country’s  10-year government bond yield 
rising above 5%. The risk of stress spreading to Italy and Spain 
adds to regional concerns, although current evidence does not 
establish a broad European fiscal crisis. German government bonds 
should benefit from safe-haven demand, while fiscal concerns keep 
risk premi a on vulnerable countries' bonds elevated. EUR/USD 
remains more sensitive to US-Germany interest-rate differentials 
and broader market confidence than to French bond yields alone. 
France’s fiscal risks, e levated energy costs and high US interest 
rates weigh on the EUR in the near term. Over the next 12 months, 
Germany’s multi -year public investment programme, resilient 
exports and sound banking-sector fundamentals are expected to 
support Euro area growth. We expect the Fed's rate-hiking cycle to 
peak by mid -2027, reducing USD support and driving  the EUR  
towards 1.17-1.18 . 
The principal tail risk  is fiscal stress widening government bond 
yield spreads and spilling over into bank funding  conditions and 
lending activity, weakening growth and delaying the EUR's recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

## Document 387: Top of Mind - Assessing a Less Transparent Fed

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind - Assessing a Less Transparent Fed.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs (Top of Mind)
Date: 2026-08-25
Pages: 26
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh is steering the Fed into a less transparent era. So, is that
a positive or negative development, and what could it mean for markets and the
economy? We speak with Former Fed governors Donald Kohn and Stephen Miran
as well as GS’ Jan Hatzius who agree that rigid “Odyssean” forward guidance that
commits policymakers to a pre-set path risks policy mistakes and is therefore rarely
appropriate. But they somewhat disagree about the benefits of great er transparency

around the Fed’s reaction function. Hatzius and Kohn argue that such transparency
helps markets better anticipate and price in policy changes, accelerating monetary
policy transmission. But Miran believes that the line between committing to a path
and being transparent is clearer in theory than in practice. And while GS economists
and strategists find that reduc ed transparency leads to increased market volatility,
the question is whether this just increases the market’s noise or also improves the

market’s signal. Hatzius argues largely for the former and Miran for the latter.
Jan Hatzius, Chief Economist and Head of Global Investment Research, Goldman Sachs
“Markets price what they think the Fed will do, not what they think the Fed should do. That will remain true even if the Fed
obscures its reaction function… market guesses about the Fed’s next moves will simply become worse, leading to more
volatility... and importantly, volatility that serves no constructive economic purpose.”

Donald Kohn, former Vice Chairman of the Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System, Robert V . Roosa
Chair in International Economics and Senior Fellow, Brookings Institution
“I sympathize with Chairman Warsh to some extent. [But] the real problem with Warsh’s approach is his reluctance to articulate
how the FOMC sees the economy evolving… explaining the Fed’s thinking about the economic outlook is essential in helping
markets act as a stabilizing force and is a core component of accountability.”

ISSUE 151 | August 19, 2026 | 1:30 PM EDT
ASSESSING A LESS TRANSPARENT FED
INTERVIEWS with…
ALSO inside…
Transparency: more benefits than costs  |  Joseph Briggs and Megan Peters, GS Global Economics Research
A less transparent Fed and US rates   |  William Marshall, GS Rates Strategy Research
Fed communication and FX volatility   |  Michael Cahill and Lexi Kanter, GS FX Strategy Research
Fed: more transparent than most   |  Joseph Briggs and Megan Peters, GS Global Economics Research

mentions by GS analysts are illustrative and should not be interpreted as investment recommendations.

Datapoints/trends we’re focused on
• US consumer spending, which we expect t o slow, with risks
skewed to the downside as the Strait of Hormuz remains
closed and further rises in gas prices would hurt consumers.
• US labor market; following weak July payroll growth,  we
estimate that the underlying pace of job growth now stands
at 5k, well below our estimate of breakeven job growth.
• US inflation, which has improved meaningfully in the past
two months, leaving us comfortable with our view that the
Fed will remain on hold through the end of the year.
Latest GS proprietary datapoints/major changes in views 
• No major changes in views.
Datapoints/trends we’re focused on
• Recent US-Japan coordinated Yen intervention, which we
think is unlikely to sustainably strengthen the Yen.
• BoJ policy; we expect the BoJ to continue hiking gradually,
with the next hike in January 2027, but the risks are skewed
toward an earlier rate hike, potentially as early as October.
• Japan consumption tax cut; while we think the tax cut will
have an only limited impact on growth, it could materially
worsen Japan’s longer-term debt outlook.

### Page 3

El
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 151

New Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh is steering the Fed into a less 
transparent era characterized by shorter post-meeting 
statements, less forward guidance, and a more limited role for 
projections, marking a meaningful break from the 
transparency revolution that has defined central banking over 
the last quarter century. So, what could a less transparent Fed 
mean for markets and the economy? 
For answers, we turn to former Fed governors Donald Kohn, 
now Robert V. Roosa Chair in International Economics and 
senior fellow at the Brookings Institution, and Stephen Miran, 
now senior strategist at Hudson Bay Capital Management, as 
well as Jan Hatzius, GS’ Chief Economist and Head of Global 
Investment Research. 
We first ask whether scaling back Fed communication is a 
positive or negative development. Hatzius says it’s likely 
negative, though he draws a clear line between transparency 
and forward guidance. He argues that clarity about the Fed’s 
reaction function helps markets better anticipate policy 
changes and quickly incorporate new information into financial 
conditions, thereby accelerating monetary policy transmission 
to the economy. So, he believes that unwinding that 
transparency would be a mistake. (Note: GS Global 
Economists Joseph Briggs and Megan Peters lay out the 
evidence backing this argument on pgs. 14-15.) 
But Hatzius takes a more nuanced view of forward guidance, 
arguing that rigid “Odyssean” guidance that commits 
policymakers to a pre-set path regardless of new information 
only makes sense at the effective lower bound for the policy 
rate, while conditional “Delphic” guidance tied to the 
economic outlook is generally more defensible. Although 
markets could confuse the two, he believes that most 
sophisticated investors understand the difference and is 
therefore not particularly worried that markets have become 
overly reliant on the policy dots published in the Fed’s 
Summary of Economic Projections (SEP). Instead, he 
considers them a “useful source of additional information 
about the Fed’s reaction function” (except for the long-run 
policy dot). If anything, Hatzius would prefer the dots move 
in an even more informative direction—by publishing each 
policymaker’s combined projections for growth, inflation, 
unemployment, and the policy rate on an anonymized basis. 
Kohn agrees that clarity about the Fed’s “narrative” and 
reaction function is vital. Explaining how the Fed sees the 
economy evolving and how policy would respond, he says, is 
“essential in helping markets act as a stabilizing force and is 
a core component of accountability.” Still, he sympathizes 
somewhat with Chairman Warsh’s view that the dot plot is 
“not helpful” (see pg. 9 for Warsh’s own words on this), 
arguing that the market’s hyper-focus on the SEP’s median 
policy dot “illustrates the risk of excessive precision and 
false certainty,” as small shifts in it can trigger unwarranted 
market moves. So, he has long favored omitting the median. 
Miran, though, is more supportive of Warsh’s push for a less 
transparent Fed. Like Hatzius and Kohn, he’s concerned that 
excessive forward guidance that locks the Fed into a policy 
path raises the risk of policy errors and also argues that such 
guidance can impair market pricing of risk. But he’s less 
convinced about the benefits of increased transparency 
around the Fed’s reaction function, making the case that the 
distinction between the Fed committing to a pre-set policy 
path and being transparent about its reaction function is clear 
in theory, but less so in practice. So, he thinks “the policy dots 
absolutely have to go” but feels less strongly about omitting 
the SEP’s economic projections. 
But would less transparency allow markets to provide the Fed 
with more “direct and unfiltered” information about the 
economy, as Warsh has argued? The answer seems to boil 
down to the implications for market volatility, and whether any 
resulting volatility would simply increase the market’s noise or 
also improve its signal. 
As for the volatility implications, the evidence is fairly clear. 
Briggs and Peters find that increased central bank 
transparency has led to a reduction in market volatility over the 
last several decades. This is consistent with GS Head of US 
Rates Strategy William Marshall’s recent observation of the 
reverse: that the US rates market has already priced 
substantial uncertainty associated with a less transparent Fed, 
with the July FOMC representing the largest non-cut meeting 
day surprise in almost three decades.  
But Marshall argues that whether such meeting-day surprises 
lead to structurally higher rates volatility depends (again) on 
whether the Fed’s reaction function is well understood. If it’s 
not, he would expect higher rates volatility to persist. GS FX 
Strategists Michael Cahill and Lexi Kanter find a similar 
pattern in FX: that central bank communication shifts can 
change which events and data drive FX volatility without 
necessarily raising it, but less transparency could increase 
Dollar volatility—all else equal (though global macro 
conditions remain its biggest driver).  
But the key debate seems to be whether the increased 
volatility resulting from less Fed transparency will improve or 
worsen the market’s signal. Hatzius is clearly in the “worsen” 
camp. He argues that markets price what they think the Fed 
will do—not what it should do—and less transparency won’t 
change that; it would simply produce worse guesses. So, he 
says that while volatility in and of itself is not necessarily bad, 
volatility driven by uncertainty about the Fed’s reaction 
function would serve no constructive economic purpose. 
Kohn agrees that the lack of a clear Fed narrative could create 
damaging uncertainty. But he argues that removing overly 
specific guidance could help free markets to process new 
information more independently and isn’t too worried about 
modestly higher volatility as a result. And Miran seems most 
convinced that reducing transparency would improve the 
markets’ signal, with volatility an unavoidable but acceptable 
cost: “The first best world of signal without volatility doesn't 
exist. I'd rather have that signal, even if it's messy, than 
throw the signal out by anchoring market expectations to the 
Fed's near-term projections.”
